This is what the hon members of this House should subscribe to as a conviction and as a principle, namely to avoid these kinds of words which really do harm rather than good.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: If the hon the Minister of the Budget has referred to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council as indulging in untruths, I submit that that is unparliamentary.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon the Minister of the Budget say that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council indulges in untruths?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I did not say the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council indulged in untruths. I made reference to what a person would do as a result of a figment of his imagination, and Hansard will bear me out. I did not refer to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>I did not say you; you did. I referred to what a man would do, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Minister may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I think the hon the Chief Whip on the other side of the House should pay more attention to what I have to say. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I want to refer very briefly to the coalition agreement between Solidarity and the NPP. I sided with the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council when that agreement was broken because I stood by an agreement that was entered into between the NPP and Solidarity. I left Solidarity to join the Ministers&#x2019; Council because I was honour bound to be with the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But who opposed you?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Whatever the case, time heals all wounds. There may be a bit of a scar left, but the scar does not do any harm. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I want to repeat that I find it absolutely essential for me to rebut some of the things that have been said by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>You are not on trial.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>I am not on trial but there are certain allegations which I need to clear up. I want to conclude by saying that in public life one has to portray honour, sincerity, dignity and integrity, and God blesses each individual with these qualities. Therefore, in view of the fact that this motion calls for the immediate resignation of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, he needs to consider this very, very seriously. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Moreover, I also know what the implications of this will be. We have studied what the constitutional requirements are, or what follows in terms of the Constitution if such a motion is passed. We also realise that the hon the State President will do what he, in his opinion, thinks is best. Therefore, we need not have any fear as to what will happen to us collectively. [Time expired.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, first of all I must compliment the hon the Minister of the Budget for maintaining such a high standard in this debate and for the dignity with which he conducted himself. I am disappointed in my hon colleagues who have decided to defect, especially <span class="col_10821-10822" refersTo="page_0841"/>those Ministers of State who are actually officebearers of Parliament.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>It is a new party, not a defection.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Well, you have defected from the NPP. [Interjections.] I am disappointed that they chose this course of action. I do not know what they want to achieve. The crossing of the floor by hon members of this House is the order of the day rather than the exception. An hon member should only cross the floor or defect from a party in a very serious situation and not just as a trivial thing that is done on a daily basis.</p>

<p>The hon Minister of the Budget implied that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council protected them. I want to confirm unequivocally that he did protect them on many occasions. The article he referred to which appeared in the <i>Sunday Times Extra</i> refers to the situation after the group broke away, not before that. Those kind of articles did not appear when he was still a member of the NPP. I want to state here that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council went out of his way, especially to protect the hon the Minister of the Budget, particularly after the coalition collapsed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>You rang me at 12h30 at night to join the NPP.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Mr Chairman, I was one of the first people to telephone the hon the Minister, because I respected him and I still do. However, I am disappointed that he did not reciprocate. His own colleagues who sit with him are the ones who wanted him removed as a Minister &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I would like to request the hon member for Moorcross to refer to hon members either as hon members or hon Ministers and not &#x201C;he&#x201D; and &#x201C;him&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Mr Chairman. I want to reiterate that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was protecting the hon the Minister of the Budget at all times. Strong demands were made to us to have the hon the Minister removed, but the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council placed his party at stake to defend the hon the Minister. In the light of this I am disappointed with his actions. However, I am confident that the situation will change shortly and that he will be back with us on this side of the House.</p>

<p>Hon members must realise that this alliance is one of convenience and anything based on convenience does not last, especially so with this alliance.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>It will be a 60-day wonder.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>They have plans for the various phases of the implementation of this. My prediction is that the alliance will collapse in a few weeks time. Yes, they did have one meeting with a certain group of people at night and another meeting with another group of people during the day. All kinds of positions have been promised to various people. The problems will come when certain aspirants are disappointed in respect of positions.</p>

<p>I want to state here unequivocally that hon members on this side of the House have full confidence in the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and all the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I therefore move as a further amendment:</p>

<block name="quote">To omit all the words after &#x201C;That&#x201D; and to substitute &#x201C;the House has full confidence in the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and all the members in the Ministers&#x2019; Council.&#x201D;</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>What about the hon member Mr Nowbath&#x2019;s motion?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is yet another gimmick.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! In terms of Rule 128(c), the amendment is allowed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: The hon member for Cavendish stated that this is another gimmick. I take umbrage to the remark. This matter should be treated with all the seriousness which it deserves. I do not think a flippant remark like that is necessary.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! For the sake of good order, will the hon member for Cavendish kindly withdraw that remark?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I withdraw that word.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, in view of the fact that a further amendment has been submitted which serves essentially the same purpose <span class="col_10823-10824" refersTo="page_0842"/>as my amendment, I should like to have that amendment withdrawn. I take it that the House will give me leave to do that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I raise an objection to that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Camperdown has raised an objection. As there is an objection to the request of the hon member Mr Nowbath, his request can unfortunately not be acceded to.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member is making a mockery of Parliament!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_health_services_and_welfare">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF HEALTH SERVICES AND WELFARE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want to start with the amendment that was proposed by the hon member for Moorcross. It seems that we have suddenly regained full confidence after the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council mentioned how poorly his Ministers have acted. He did not mention my name, but he did indicate some of the shortfalls of the Ministers. I am pleased to note that there has been a rethink. I believe that there has been a meeting until a quarter to two this morning to try and work out a plan to reconcile the situation.</p>

<p>However, the very fact that four Ministers have resigned from the NPP, indicates that there must be a lack of confidence somewhere along the line. I believe that as responsible Ministers we have not made any hasty decisions. We have considered our actions very carefully, and I believe that hon members have made every effort to work in the best interest of the community and country at large. However, we reached a stage where 11 hon members of the NPP became so frustrated that it resulted in a new party coming into being.</p>

<p>Hon members have tried from every angle to see how they could work together, but it is a hard fact that they have now formed a new political party. What annoys hon members&#x2014;and Ministers, in particular&#x2014;is that Ministers are discussed at every party branch meeting. When responsible Ministers&#x2019; positions are discussed and they are called lame ducks by colleagues, it makes for a very frustrating situation.</p>

<p>I am aware of the fact that this sort of thing has taken place at more than one meeting. When one is dealing with ordinary people, some of whom are opportunists and hang around purely for positions they seek, it is most unbecoming that Ministers are discussed in such a manner.</p>

<p>There is another issue that concerns me deeply. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has denied that he said anything about dangling carrots. When one finds that carrots are being dangled&#x2014;and I believe there are six news reporters who can substantiate that these were the exact words used by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council&#x2014;it gives one food for thought. It is a fact that carrots were dangled to many people.</p>

<p>The 11 hon members who have formed the new political party, have stood solid in order to try and create a new image for the House of Delegates. I believe that we are responsible people. We are people who have fought elections. We have constituencies that we have to look after. The type of allegations that have been made with regard to bribery, corruption and what not, has not enhanced the image of this House.</p>

<p>I think we have taken a very bold stand and I would like to say, as a Minister in this House, that I was quite prepared to put my head on a block and say, come what may, irrespective of what happens I will hopefully be able to play some role in trying to maintain the image of this particular House. Having served as the Chairman of the first House for the past two and a half years, I had great joy in working with hon members and I think we have built up a happy relationship with all hon members. When these type of things come to light it makes one very frustrated. When I had fought my election some time back in 1984 like all of us did, while I stood as member of the NPP, political parties did not mean anything at all at that time. Each and everyone us won our elections on our track records. The threat of an election is something that does not worry me one bit. I am quite prepared to say that because of our track records, we can win the election. I think, however, that we must take into consideration the fact that the hon the State President, irrespective of anything that has been said, cannot afford to have an election. I am not afraid an election, but the hon the State President cannot afford to have an election of this nature where we have to have an election in 1989. Our colleagues in the House of Representatives have made it very clear that they will not bow until the Group Areas Act is repealed. As far as that is concerned, we are now told about the 60-day wonder by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. My colleague, <span class="col_10825-10826" refersTo="page_0843"/>the hon member for Moorcross had indicated and predicted that the alliance will collapse, but I think we are reasonable minded people serving here and I think the ultimate aim is to regain an image for the House of Delegates.</p>

<p>Numerous newspapers have indicated the question of elections and so forth. I think one should not be afraid or scared of elections, because if the notice of motion by the hon member Mr Nowbath is approved, or adopted by this House, an election will have to be called. I think the hon member Mr Nowbath in his own wisdom has taken 24 hours to realise what a blunder he made. [Interjections.] The hon member Mr Nowbath has never fought an election so he probably does not realise that he will not win another election. The hon member for Moorcross has moved a further amendment that he has full confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council which I sincerely appreciate because as a team we work extremely well together. In this regard I think I should make my position perfectly clear and that is that I do no represent a political party in my Ministry. I think hon members on all sides of the House can vouch for that. My Ministry deals mostly with bread-and-butter issues and I have treated each and every member, whatever the request, as members of Parliament. I will continue to do so as long as I am in office.</p>

<p>I also want to refer to the question of disability grants. Nobody has any right to say anything about the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council as far as the cancellation of disability grants are concerned. I do realize, of course, that allegations were made, but I take full responsibility for this aspect. The question of disability grants is decided and determined by a panel of professional people. I do not believe there should be any political interference. I will certainly not interfere by a political decision, but rather by a professional decision. Unfortunately a lot has already been said about it and I have taken heed of this. I have taken the necessary steps to try and obviate such repetition. In fairness to my colleagues, including the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, I must say that we have worked harmoniously. Each and everyone of us are doing our job with dedication and enthusiasm.</p>

<p>Some of us can complete our work in four hours. The others take eight hours, but I want to say all of us leave our offices with a clean desk. We are ready for the next day. Like a Scout we are always prepared and ready for any eventuality.</p>

<p>With regard to the question the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council mentioned concerning a seven day working week I want to say it is quite true. As a colleague I want to say that he is spending far too much time on his work and he certainly is neglecting his family. It is a fact. I believe as someone who is a hard-working Minister that he should deal with his time in the way all of us do. We are all human beings and we are only capable of working so much. However, one will find that Ministers are on call 24 hours a day seven days a week and I think within reason all of us are duty bound to do our work.</p>

<p>With these words I want to say that I have full confidence in my hon colleagues and I am pleased that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and the Chief Whip have confirmed that they have full confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council. [Time expired.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want to raise a question with the hon member&#x2019;s of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Whilst we note the motions that have been submitted here and whilst I am not going to dispute the conscientious objections that they are raising now as it is their right to do so, I nevertheless want to ask why use this forum now? Why did they not raise all of this during the last four years in the Ministers&#x2019; Council? How is it that they were able to keep quiet in the Ministers&#x2019; Council? Why are they now coming along here and saying that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was an autocratic dictator and that the point of no return is now reached? They now form a new party and then to use the floor of this House to make allegations against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers Council. I am sure they had enough muscle to do what they wanted to do inside the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p>Every year, if one looks at Hansard, they praised the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Each and everyone of them praised him. I was reading in my office the submissions put forward by the hon member for Camperdown earlier this year in the no-confidence debate where he quoted &#x201C;certain Ministers&#x201D; verbatim during the motion of no confidence in 1986 when some members crossed the floor and opposed members of the NPP. Then in 1987 the same members opposed Solidarity.</p>

<p><span class="col_10827-10828" refersTo="page_0844"/>Now we find ourselves in this difficult situation which they call &#x201C;changing circumstances&#x201D; and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council calls &#x201C;fluctuating circumstances&#x201D; in a certain debate. I do not know what is happening. It seems as if every month there are changes and fluctuations and today hon Ministers are using the floor of Parliament to air their grievances. I now ask them why did they not air their grievances when they had the muscle? Why did they not have the courage of their convictions to take the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council to task. Were they afraid they would be fired or that something would happen to them?</p>

<p>What happened to them to cause them to cross the floor? What happened to them that they had to join a new party?</p>

<p>I want to refer to the words of the responsible hon the Deputy Minister of Environment Affairs who is also the hon the Deputy Minister of Local Government, Housing and Agriculture in the House of Delegates. I quote from Hansard, 20 May 1987, col 77:</p>

<block name="quote">I have been encouraged the last day or two by the numerous telephone calls that have come to me in support of the stand I took in leaving the National People&#x2019;s Party and declaring myself an independent and aligning myself with the Solidarity Party. These calls of congratulation and support have come from people of substance who have influence in the community. I do not want to embarrass them by naming them, but I have had people telephoning me. They are people of high standing. I respect them. I have had calls from foreign embassies, congratulating me and saying that the people who left the National People&#x2019;s Party deserved the support of the country because &#x2026;</block>

<p>At the time the hon the Deputy Minister was just an ordinary member. In the next column he says in an answer to an interjection:</p>

<block name="quote">No, I do not get lost. I know where I am. There are suggestions made by ill-informed people that the former Minister of Housing&#x2014;he was thrown out of Housing &#x2026;</block>

<p>He goes on to say that he does not fall for carrots. However, when he was appointed Deputy Minister he went back to the NPP and now he is back on the other side again. Are these &#x201C;fluctuating circumstances&#x201D;?</p>

<p>How do we vote today? How do we know who to support? How do we know that that alliance will not break up next week? [Interjections.] That hon member must please keep quiet. I did not make interjections when he spoke. I am only asking a question. The hon member can only ask questions through the Chair. Does the hon member understand that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Rylands did not interject when any other hon member spoke. Hon members must please give him a hearing.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>Thank you, Mr Chairman. We are asked here to decide on a very important matter. I want to point out that it is of no use to tell us of &#x201C;fluctuating circumstances.&#x201D; Even the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is guilty of using that term. One has to be consistent where these things are concerned.</p>

<p>What worries me, is that it is the same people who shower praise upon him every year. The hon member for Reservoir Hills attacked the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I hold no brief for the hon member for Reservoir Hills. Hon members used to laugh at the hon member for Reservoir Hills and make interjections when he spoke.</p>

<p>As the draft resolution is worded, it is possible that the hon the State President may appoint as Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council whoever enjoys the support of the majority of this House. It is also possible that he may be appointed as an hon member of the Cabinet. I now want to put a question to hon members and specifically to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition.</p>

<p>They have been saying that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is a stooge because he defends Cabinet policy and the emergency regulations and he is also a junior partner in the National Party. Will the next chairman become the junior partner? Will he support the emergency regulations? Will he support the regional services councils?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member for Rylands use words to the effect that some hon members said that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is a stooge?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Mr Chairman, I said <span class="col_10829-10830" refersTo="page_0845"/>that they called him that. I do not call him that. They called him that in previous debates.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I think that the hon member for Rylands must withdraw the word &#x201C;stooge&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>With respect, Sir, I abide by your request, and I withdraw the word.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>The dilemma is this. The Official Opposition has its standpoint, guided by its official policy as determined by its congress. According to the courage of their convictions they have strongly objected and that is their right. Now, however, how are they going to continue if they sit on this side of the line? They will be seen to be defending Government policy. [Interjections.] They will be seen defending that which they oppose. Then we will again be caught in a Catch-22 situation because this side of the House will become the opposition and one will see a tug-of-war taking place once again. Therefore I will again be listening when this is said: &#x201C;Circumstances have now changed &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Fluctuating circumstances.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026;and therefore it is a matter of fluctuating circumstances. We, being now the ruling party, have to defend the National Party. We have now become the junior partners of the Nationalist Party&#x201D;.</p>

<p>I therefore firmly submit that the time has come for the hon the State President to dissolve this House and that we should hold an election, and let the community decide who should represent the people.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Rylands Estate.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>That is the choice of Rylands. You are one of those that submitted earlier that we should go for elections&#x2014;now have the courage of your convictions to stand by that resolution. You are not going to push me around in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#deputy_minister_of_local_government_housing_and_agriculture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">DEPUTY MINISTER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT, HOUSING AND AGRICULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>On a point of order, Mr Chairman: I ask with respect whether the hon member for Rylands is allowed to refer to other hon members as &#x201C;you&#x201D;? I heard him refer to the hon member for Stanger as &#x201C;you&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I would like to remind hon members that in the heat of this debate hon members may tend to use certain words that they should not. I should like to appeal to hon members to refer to other hon members as &#x201C;hon members&#x201D;. The hon member may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#manikkam">

<from>Rev <person refersTo="hansard_za">E J MANIKKAM</person>:</from>

<p>My only advice to the hon members who interject is that I have the right to submit what I want to submit, and there is no point in turning around and saying &#x201C;Rylands Estate&#x201D;. I could turn around and say that the Stanger constituency can decide whether they want to re-elect the hon member who represents them now. What I am submitting is this: That this House, because of changing, fluctuating circumstances that are occurring month by month&#x2014;and now it is going to be week by week&#x2014;should go to the electorate to enable the electorate to make up its mind as to what to do with this House and elect representatives who will serve the best interests of the country and address national issues.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it is the most painful task for me this afternoon to have to say certain things, under constraint. However, I do so because of the lesson that the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council mentioned yesterday as regards the lessons taught to humanity on the fields of Kurukshetra, where Krishna advises Arjuna to take up the cudgels and fight against his own people to put right things that were wrong, and to uphold righteousness and dharma. It is therefore in that vein that I rise to speak this afternoon.</p>

<p>First of all I want to clear up this matter raised by the hon member for Rylands who said that for four years he was there and why did we not have enough muscle to stand up and clarify this position. I want to tell him in absolutely clear terms that in the Ministers&#x2019; Council there is a word that is mutilated, and that word is &#x201C;discipline&#x201D;.</p>

<p>If one goes beyond a certain stage in discussions, one is disciplined. One can go no further than that. Even if one is discussing the matter properly, the standing phrase is: &#x201C;Let me tell you&#x201D;, and then one is guillotined. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I want to say this afternoon that I was the first one, together with my colleague on the other side of the House, the hon the Minister of Local <span class="col_10831-10832" refersTo="page_0846"/>Government and Agriculture, to approach the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council when we heard rumblings, in order to discuss the matter with him.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#deputy_minister_of_local_government_housing_and_agriculture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">DEPUTY MINISTER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT, HOUSING AND AGRICULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>He shared the pain.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_education_and_culture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND CULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, we shared the pain, but we were told: &#x201C;No, no, there is nothing in it&#x201D;. The impression I gained was that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was a know-all and that therefore he did not want any advice from anyone else. [Interjections.] I was terribly sorry when I was called in at another meeting at which the hon member for Isipingo was present and he said point blank to the chairman: &#x201C;We are looking for nobody else&#x2019;s head but yours&#x201D;. It pained me very much. [Interjections.] However, I am giving hon members the facts.</p>

<p>Discipline is one thing; discussions are another. When one is discussing matters, one must be given the freedom of discussion at all times, and there was no freedom insofar as that was concerned.</p>

<p>In my last job I was the principal of a secondary school with 54 people on my staff. These were people from all religious denominations and there was not so much as a rumble from them during the four years in which I exercised control as head of that department. It is a great pity that a person controlling a few, should have so much dissension.</p>

<p>I want to say that I am dedicated to the cause of education but I am not free to carry out all those things that I want to do for the pupils of my community, for the education of our people and the administration &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>May I ask the hon the Minister a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>No, thank you. I only have a few minutes. [Interjections]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But when you crucified us in the Committee of Education Ministers, I backed you. Admit that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has backed me. He has also embarrassed me on many occasions. [Interjections.] I was terribly embarrassed during the elections in Tongaat. The hon member for Tongaat will know that I was a Minister of Education and someone else was telling them that teachers could take leave. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, if I had simple things to do, I could have done them. I could have maintained the image of the Ministry as well as that of the department as a whole. However, certain people find it convenient to exceed the bounds of propriety, and if we objected we were shown the door. That was painful enough. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I also gained the impression that because I was doing my duty well and because I knew education inside out, having been a teacher in the primary school and in the secondary school, a lecturer at the training college, and a principal of both primary and secondary schools, I was beginning to steal the thunder. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I regret to interrupt the hon the Minister but the time allowed to his party in this debate has expired.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I do not have a written speech. [Interjections.] I do not have a written speech and therefore my time is unlimited but I shall speak off the cuff. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I am going to speak from the heart here today. [Interjections.] Mr Chairman, I did not interrupt anyone. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I want to appeal to hon members to permit the hon member for Brickfield to proceed with his speech without interruptions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Thank you, Sir. I do not want to discuss the hon Ministers or why they have left and are sitting on that side. They know for themselves. However, I regret that their complaints and grievances were never discussed at the caucus. [Interjections.] They were never discussed. If one&#x2019;s finger is cut, one does not cut one&#x2019;s arm or head. One treats the wound.</p>

<p>An important question is: Why did this come about? How did this jumping over from here to there and from there to here come about? On what grounds did it take place?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Dissatisfaction.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from><span class="col_10833-10834" refersTo="page_0847"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Dissatisfaction! I will say&#x2014;and I speak under oath&#x2014;what the hon member for Isipingo told me. I will say it to his face. He said: &#x201C;Mr Khan, Uncle Cha-cha, how long do you want a Hindu to lead? Please sit with us. How long do you want a Hindu to lead you?&#x201D; [Interjections.] I am speaking under oath.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I think the hon member &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Does the hon member have a point of order or a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>He is on record for telling lies in court.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member for Isipingo say that the hon member for Brickfield is telling lies?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>No Mr Chairman. He is on record in court. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member for Isipingo repeat himself?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Brickfield is on record in a courtcase where the magistrate found him guilty of lying. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I think the hon member for Isipingo must withdraw it, because he does not have those facts here to substantiate it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member for Isipingo withdraw it?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Brickfield may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I speak here &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member for Brickfield take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am taking no questions, Mr Chairman. Hon members can write to me and I will reply. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is it true that the hon member for Brickfield had hon members locked up as a result of &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Brickfield must proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the truth hurts. The hon member for Isipingo asked me: &#x201C;Cha-cha, if you die, who will carry you?&#x201D; [Interjections.] Hon members must please listen to me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>We do not want to listen to rubbish.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>You do not want to listen to rubbish? You speak that too.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I would like to remind the hon member for Stanger that the Chair will decide whether it is rubbish or not. I would like to appeal to hon members for their co-operation. The hon member for Brickfield gave all other hon members a hearing. He is now entitled to a hearing as well.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: The hon member for Brickfield said: &#x201C;I will have you locked up.&#x201D; I would like to ask the Chair that he withdraws it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! To whom did the hon member for Brickfield refer?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, he said that to the hon member for Isipingo. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member for Brickfield say to the hon member for Isipingo that he would have him locked up?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Mr Chairman, I did say that. I am prepared to withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Brickfield must please withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Apologise!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, what did the hon member for Isipingo tell me? He said, &#x201C;I am going to pilgrimage, but before I hit that Shatahn, before I hit those three devils there, I will hit this devil&#x201D;. He said he wanted to get the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council out of this place. Why are hon members here?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from><span class="col_10835-10836" refersTo="page_0848"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, is the hon member prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I want to tell hon members that the hon member for Brickfield has made it quite clear that he will not be taking any questions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, to get rid of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, has nothing to do with religious grounds. What did the hon member for Isipingo tell me when I asked him who his leader was? He said: &#x201C;Chamcha, J N Reddy.&#x201D;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I once more want to request hon members to refer to hon members as hon members. Dr Reddy cannot be referred to as J N Reddy. He is the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I only quoted what the hon member for Isipingo had said to me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member must refer to hon members of the House as hon members.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>He said that his leader was the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. However, he said that it was only a temporary measure, because the hon the Minister of the Budget would be the leader.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>That is a lie!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon the Minister calling me a liar?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Minister of the Budget shouted that it is a lie. Will he please withdraw that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I withdraw it, but the hon member for Brickfield is a liar! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Minister of the Budget must withdraw the word &#x201C;liar&#x201D; unconditionally.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I respect your wishes. I withdraw the words that I used when I said that the hon member for Brickfield is a liar.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am now coming to the hon the Minister. Sir, one can speak lies if one wants to, but one has to face one&#x2019;s Creator one day. What did the hon member for Isipingo tell me?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Brickfield has said that one can tell lies today, but that one has to face one&#x2019;s Creator one day. Has any hon member here spoken a lie in the opinion of the hon member?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>No, Sir.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member kindly withdraw those words?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I withdraw them. When one speaks the truth, it hurts. I want the hon member for Isipingo to deny what I have said here. I want him to deny that it is not the truth. I do not benefit from telling lies against the hon member.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Why did you have to go to court then?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>I went to court, and the hon the Minister knows why I went to court. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Because you told a lie!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I appeal to hon members to refrain from calling other hon members liars. The hon the Minister of the Budget has again used the word liar. Will he kindly withdraw that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will hon members please refrain from making similar remarks?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member for Isipingo has denied that he has said anything of this sort. I said to him I am not a chamcha. I am a breyani spoon, a long one. [Time expired.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I should like to react to the last two speakers. [Interjections.] If those hon members will permit me to, I should like first to react to the hon member for Rylands and then the hon member for Brickfield.</p>

<p>The hon member for Rylands made great play of a very simple phrase, namely, changing circumstances and fluctuating situations. [Interjections.] <span class="col_10837-10838" refersTo="page_0849"/>Mr Chairman, people living in glass houses should not throw stones.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I request the hon member for Isipingo kindly to contain himself. The hon member for Camperdown may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I repeat, Sir, that people living in glass houses should not throw stones. The hon member for Rylands won his election on a Solidarity ticket. After he had been elected to the Chair, he went independent and then, in order to save his position as Chairman, he joined the NPP. And who used the election to proclaim the great philosophy of unity and loyalty? Mr Chairman, I consider everything that that hon member had to say in this House today as being cosmetic. It was done to save his face.</p>

<p>I want to come now to the hon member for Brickfield. It is very difficult for me to do so, but I am constrained to say now what I must say. When a person is found guilty of fraud in a court of law in this country, how does that person or anybody else at all expect us to believe all the nonsense he has spoken in this House today? [Interjections.] It is on record in the court in Natal that this particular person was found guilty of fraud. [Interjections.] If I am found guilty of fraud I will leave this House. I will not take my seat in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I please ask the hon member a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>No, Sir, I will not take any questions now.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I should like to comment briefly on section 39 (3) of the Constitution. I refer here particularly to paragraph (b) which affects this House. Great play has been made of a particular phrase in this debate up till now to which I vainly tried to close my ears. However, all we have heard has been &#x201C;election, election, election&#x201D;. [Interjections.] Section 39 (3) (b) states quite clearly&#x2014;</p>

<block name="quote">the State President shall so dissolve any House or reconstitute the Ministers&#x2019; Council in question if&#x2014;</block>

<ol>

<li>(i) such House passes a motion of non-confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council in question;</li>

</ol>

<p>I want to point out, Sir, that in this case it is not the Ministers&#x2019; Council that is being castigated; it is the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council himself, an individual. Section 39 (3) (b) goes on to state that the hon the State President shall so dissolve any House or reconstitute the Ministers&#x2019; Council in question if such House rejects any Bill which is a money Bill.</p>

<p>If any person tries to tell this House or anybody at all that the hon the State President will construe a motion of no-confidence in an individual as a motion covered by the relevant section of the Constitution to which I have just referred, then he will be insulting the integrity and the intelligence of the hon the State President.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Nobody said that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>It was said by implication. It infers that the hon the State President is unable to use his judgement. If one member of any team of six is particularly singled out and penalised for foul play, the entire team is not disbanded. In my soccer-playing days, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was a referee. [Interjections.] During the period in which the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council was a referee, when he had to discipline a player for, let us say, kicking another player, did he then suspend the entire team? Would he have done that all on account of one player? [Interjections.] This motion before the House moved by the Official Opposition and subsequently amended by the hon the Deputy Minister singles out the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council as the guilty party. It does not cast any aspersions on him; it merely states that he has lost the support of the majority in this House. Because he does not command that majority any longer, they say that the conventions demand that he resign.</p>

<p>They were harping so much on what convention demands, but if I were the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council I would have nobly resigned my position and accepted the inevitable. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Convention is that one ought to enjoy the support of the majority in this House. This has been proved beyond doubt to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. He had from the 5 May to 19 May&#x2014;he was deliberately allowed that leeway&#x2014;to test the validity of this new conciliation party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>A convenience party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from><span class="col_10839-10840" refersTo="page_0850"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>No, it is an alliance party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>What party do you belong to?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>To my grandmother&#x2019;s party!</p>

<p>That proves that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has failed to convince those members who left his party and formed another party to come back. That is ample proof that all that bluff and loose talk that this alliance will never last is just to keep those people. When one plays the game of goats things go haywire.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I enter this debate with much reluctance and with much pain. I want to reiterate what the hon the Minister of Health Service and Welfare said in his address.</p>

<p>He said that every candidate that is seated in this House, except for those who have been nominated directly or indirectly, were elected not on a party ticket but by the contribution made by each individual over the years under trying circumstances to uplift the suffering communities in the different areas of South Africa. However, what do we see today?</p>

<p>We took the oath when we were elected as members of Parliament. One talks about honesty and corruption and this and that but <i>I</i> want to say that in the Bible it says that in a village all the villagers found a certain person to be committing adultery. They took her to an open piece of ground and they gathered around her with stones to kill her. Then Christ appeared and said, &#x201C;If any of you have not sinned, cast the first stone&#x201D;. There were sinners on this world 2 000 years ago, because no one cast a stone.</p>

<p>I therefore want to tell the hon member for Camperdown that it is high time he took account of himself and what he does. It is no good throwing stones from a glass house because he himself left Solidarity on whose ticket he fought election. [Interjections.] I shall not take any questions; I did not disturb anyone when they were speaking. Then he joined another party and Solidarity expelled them. He therefore cannot join Solidarity again because the Congress adopted a resolution.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>You are talking a lot of nonsense. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I would like to remind the hon member for Camperdown that the hon member for Mariannhill did not interrupt the hon member when he was speaking.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Sir, but he is talking rubbish!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Mariannhill may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>I feel members who make statements must honour them.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: I heard the hon member for Camperdown saying that the hon member for Mariannhill is talking rubbish. Is it not unparliamentary?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Mariannhill may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>People are judged by the company they keep and by the words that they use. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The Government of this country made tremendous sacrifices so that we can come to Parliament as a part of the reform process in South Africa. I believe we should go home and reform ourselves. We have no trust or room for reconciliation or reasonable arguments amongst ourselves. If we cannot mend our differences and put our own homes in order, how can we ask the White man to trust us? I want to be honest and blunt.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#deputy_minister_of_local_government_housing_and_agriculture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">DEPUTY MINISTER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT, HOUSING AND AGRICULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>No questions, Mr Chairman.</p>

<p>I believe that if one wants to cross the floor and join another party, the honourable thing to do is to resign from Parliament and to go back to one&#x2019;s electorate to tell them what one intends to do. One must then ask them whether they agree or not. Nobody here will do that.</p>

<p>I want to say that I will stick with this party. We all have our little faults&#x2014;make no mistake about that. I also want to refer to what the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition said recently at a sad occasion. He said that one should involve oneself in activities and take steps to put things right. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council always says that lightening striks the tallest. One must not duck to avoid it. If one <span class="col_10841-10842" refersTo="page_0851"/>wants to fight a fire, one must go right up to it. One must not run away when the fire gets too hot.</p>

<p>I believe that what is happening at the moment will repeat itself. My fear is that we come here to find out what we are, what we should be and what we should not be. I agree with what the hon Chief Whip of this House said in his amendment. I certainly have the fullest confidence in the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, the hon the Ministers, the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition and everyone in this House. [Interjections.] I will be the first one to forgive hon members if they have made a mistake while doing their work&#x2014;everybody can make mistakes.</p>

<p>It is said that one must not start a fight with the fishes in the sea because one will never win. I believe that what is happening is becoming a habit amongst responsible people. I want to ask all hon members to get together and to sort out our differences once and for all. We must get together and do the work for which we are here. South Africa is going through a very difficult stage.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#deputy_minister_of_local_government_housing_and_agriculture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">DEPUTY MINISTER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT, HOUSING AND AGRICULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a reasonable question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>No, I do not want to hear anything. People have been given the opportunity in this House to fill responsible positions and to address themselves to issues of national importance but instead we are dealing with the same parochial issues over and over again. We are concerned about people who do not call us or do not even greet us while South Africa is going through great difficulties every day. How many hours a day does the hon the State President spend in doing his duty towards the country? I want to make an appeal to hon members not to acquire responsible positions and then to misuse it. I am not saying that that has happened but I appeal to hon members for the sake of our country and our people.</p>

<p>I want to make this appeal to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. I am not a stranger; it is not that I do not know him. We worked together for very many years and we have a very good track record. I want to see who is going to come and blot that out. I also wish to say that a combination in this House of men who are capable, is being written off, because we are looking in a different direction instead of looking at the directions that we should be looking in. We have directed ourselves towards the wrong cause. If anything is not going to be done in good time we shall all be driven to the point at which we find we will become useless. The credibility we had in 1983 or 1984 among the people that returned us here will be at risk; they, too, will be sitting and wondering where we are and what we are doing. While this debate is going on, senior hon members of this House&#x2014;senior not only in terms of protocol or parliamentary procedures, but in terms of age, qualifications and other things&#x2014;are just having a laugh at one another; when one says something it is a big joke.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>And they wash their dirty linen.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#naranjee">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M NARANJEE</person>:</from>

<p>Is that really the purpose for which we are here, or are we here to address ourselves to the problems? And then we want to know why reform is not going on. We should go to a reform school ourselves first and reform ourselves and then ask everyone else to bring about reform!</p>

<p>I also wish to say that we have full confidence in the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, the Ministers and every hon member of this House. We believe that they are respectable members and I have full confidence in them. [Interjections.] If there are certain shortfalls I should be the first one to say that we must assist that man to get out of that problem so that we can proceed further. It is no use blaming the engine driver and saying that one knows the engine driver is no good when one is sitting in the same train, because if that train gets derailed, one will get hurt oneself.</p>

<p>Let us, therefore, put our heads together and come to some kind of an understanding so that we can build some kind of&#x2014;shall I say&#x2014;stability in this House so that we can all do our work.</p>

<p>There are men who can do a little bit more and men who can do a little bit less, there are men who can do a lot more and there are people who can make mistakes. There are people who can lead us, and let us look to them and continue with the work that we have come here for.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I have been listening to some very emotive speeches. Some people have been trying to justify their own <span class="col_10843-10844" refersTo="page_0852"/>actions and trying to cast aspersions on other people.</p>

<p>I do not wish to respond to the comments of the hon member for Rylands Estate. I think that if he searches his own mind and his own conscience and looks at his own correspondence file containing the correspondence exchanged between the party and himself he will know exactly what these fluctuating circumstances are all about. I do not wish to reduce the level of the debate any further than has already been done by the hon member for Brickfield.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">A KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Thank you.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I now wish to deal with some of the issues here. I want to support the hon member for Reservoir Hills insofar as his approach to and concept of elections is concerned. I want hon members in this House to know, and I want the public at large to know, that we on this side of the House have no problem with elections.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>If we want leaders of credibility and we believe in the concept and principle of democracy, we must not be afraid of elections. Only those who have no contact with the constituencies will be afraid of elections.</p>

<p>I want to say quite categorically that in the circumstances in which we find ourselves in this particular House the elections must not be used as a bogey for the further dangling of carrots and for further negotiations to maintain a majority in this House to control the destiny of our people.</p>

<p>This side of the House will support an election without any hesitation or any problem.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Then support me on my amendment!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>If that hon member had supported me when I wanted a commission of enquiry in order to achieve a clean image for this House, then I would have supported his call for an election.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Do not run away with ifs and buts.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, all that hon member can do is interject; he has never faced an election and he knows nothing about elections.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>I do not have to, nor do I need to.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>We want to state quite categorically and we wish to place it on record that as soon as a commission of enquiry has been established and as soon as all the allegations levelled in this House relating to corruption in respect of land deals and education have been cleared&#x2014;and intimidation of teachers for the purpose of obtaining votes&#x2014;if all those practices are stopped and cleared, we will go for the election the following morning. It is as simple as that.</p>

<p>For the first time I have heard the hon the Minister of Education and Culture make an admission here, and I hope that the Press is listening to this, or that they heard the hon the Minister when he said that teachers were doing things in his own department and someone else was authorising it. He had no control over that situation. He made that admission with regard to the Tongaat elections. Is that not indicative of the type of malpractices that have occurred? What more evidence does anyone require? Furthermore &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>No, I am not taking any questions, thank you very much. I want to continue by saying that I can understand the situation and the circumstances in which the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council finds himself. He is fighting for his survival.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>No, no. We shall see who fights for survival.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I understand that. Whether or not he denies it, that is my opinion. [Interjections.] However, when, in that situation, he comes here and attacks those hon Ministers whom he himself appointed, or whom he recommended to the hon the State President for appointment &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member is one of the experienced hon members in the House. The hon member must please refer to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council as such.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I accept that correction, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from><span class="col_10845-10846" refersTo="page_0853"/>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, in reference to his own hon members, has said certain things in the media. This aspect has been canvassed by other speakers. I do not want to go into the details but to my mind, the point which stands out loud and clear is the fact that now, when he finds himself in this situation, it is necessary for him to denigrate his hon colleagues. If they were incompetent, then he himself was incompetent in appointing them in the first instance.</p>

<p>I also want to say that the motions that have been proposed by hon members on the other side of the House make a laughing stock of their approach. It is laughable. It is like clinging to the last straws, hope against hope. I believe that they do not know what they are doing. They are unable to understand what is going on and consequently they moved two motions which were directly the opposite of one another. Where is the problem? Where do the inconsistencies lie? Is it on this side of the House or on that side of the House? [Interjections.] When one reads those two motions, one finds that they are totally inconsistent, totally incompatible.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>That is why we attempted to withdraw one.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has been saying in the newspapers that he will have his majority by such and such a date. As a politician I am sure that he was hoping to obtain his majority. He says he is a heavy-weight. He will try. He is a good political boxer. He will try. However, I treat with contempt his statement yesterday to the effect that he could have obtained his majority the day before.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I could have done it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I do not believe that. To the last man, the hon members sitting here have indicated the opposite, both in the caucus and in this House, and despite all the persuasion and all the invitations to join the other side, they have been resolute in their stand.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Your troubles have commenced today!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Furthermore, it was suggested that if we had a vote of no-confidence in terms of section 39 of the Constitution, and its subsections, there would be a dissolution of the House. Why? Why keep making an issue of the dissolution of the House? It was said &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What are you saying&#x2014;desolation? What you are saying will lead it to desolation. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is becoming quite articulate and is quite correct in his pronunciation. He is making some progress, and I acknowledge that progress. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>As far as I am concerned, this threat about elections must come to an end. I believe that the law advisers and the hon the State President have other provisions in the Constitution to which they could make reference. Moreover, the example given here by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is not valid because if it followed automatically that the Ministers&#x2019; Council as a whole dissolved as a result of the resignation of, or the passing of a motion of no confidence in the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, then the Constitution would have been worded differently.</p>

<p>There would have been no need for two sections, section 21 and section 24. Section 21 isolates the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council from hon the Ministers. That is clear. I am not a constitutional lawyer, but it is absolutely clear.</p>

<p>Also, whilst I am on my feet, from what little I understand, I want to bring it to the attention of the hon member Mr Nowbath and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council that they must look at section 19 and the exclusions contained therein, which make it very clear as to who makes appointments. It is not the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council who appoints the Ministers of this House, but the hon the State President. It is at his pleasure that appointments are made and that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is sitting where he is sitting. This is the correct situation.</p>

<p>I can understand that my colleague on the other side, the hon member for Moorcross, was disappointed. I think he was quite truthful in saying that he was disappointed. I can understand his disappointment. He was disappointed at losing <span class="col_10847-10848" refersTo="page_0854"/>such able hon Ministers from his party. I want to acknowledge his disappointment. This again proves that those hon Ministers who left his party are in fact worth their salt.</p>

<p>One can go on and on in this debate. I now want to make reference to the hon member for Brickfield, whom I do not see in the House. Oh, he is there. I think he was to an extent exposed by way of interjections. However, I want to state quite categorically that, while the newspapers have been playing this up, I would like the media to analyse the religious backgrounds of those people who left the other side and of hon Ministers of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. This is being used as a ploy to try and divide the community along sectional lines. Sitting on this side of the House are not only Muslims. I think one must treat that allegation with the disgust and contempt it deserves. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>To accuse the hon member for Isipingo of indulging in that type of practice is absolute nonsense. I know the hon member for Isipingo, who claims that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has been his friend for the last 25 years and declares his affinity to him. It has nothing to do with religion. I think it is the height of timidity for the hon member for Brickfield to cast such aspersions on the hon member for Isipingo. I think he should re-examine the statements he made and consider very carefully what he utters in this House.</p>

<p>I also found that hon members on the other side, instead of defending the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, were in fact in a sense passing a vote of no confidence in their own leader. What did I hear in those replies? Instead of defending the role of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I do not need anybody to defend me. I will take you all at one time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Well, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council tried and he failed.</p>
